  9. Hydraulic / PDA-DDA Decision Analysis

  A. FACT / EVIDENCE. Seminar p. 53 (Tier 2, verbatim): under DDA "یاهه‌رگ همه رد
   راشف هب هجوت نودب ؛دوشی‌م نیمأت لماک روطه‌ب هراومه زایندروم یاضاقت ،هکبش
  دهد ناشن نامزم‌ه ار اضاقت لماک نیمأت ای یفنم راشف تسا نکمم DDA …دوجوم یکیلوردیه
   WNTR/EPANET 2.2 (Tier 4): Wagner PDA ".تسا داضت رد هکبش یکیزیف تیعقاو اب هک
  is the native default of both simulators; defaults are required_pressure = 
  0.07 m, minimum_pressure = 0, pressure_exponent = 0.5 "to mimic EPANET", and
  "since the default P_f is very low, leaving it unchanged causes the PDD output
  to be nearly identical to DD." Abdy Sayyed et al. 2015 (Tier 1):
  pressure-driven analysis "assesses system performance more realistically" and
  has been used in "leakage management (Tabesh et al. 2009)". Mahdavi 1400 (Tier
  1, Persian thesis, Shahid Beheshti, supervisor Dr Jafar Yazdi): PRV analysis
  with PDA + bi-objective DE on a Tehran network. This phase (Tier 1): in Negm's
  full text \bPDA\b=0, \bDDA\b=0, demand-driven=0; all four "pressure
  dependent" occurrences concern leakage, not demand. Hu MARL eq. (13): Σq_ij = 
  d_i + d_i^leaks — leakage enters mass balance as a term separate from demand.

  B. INFERENCE. The scientific reason for PDA, stated precisely: in EPANET,
  leakage flows through the emitter path and consumer demand through the PDA 
  function — two separate paths. When the agent drives pressure down, leakage
  falls per k·p^N₁ and simultaneously supplied demand d_{j,t} falls per Wagner,
  so DSR drops and "leakage reduction" cannot be faked by cutting service.

  What is lost under DDA: this mechanism disappears entirely. d ≡ D always, DSR
  is always 100 %, and the only brake left is the violation term — which in
  Negm's case was binary, leaving the agent indifferent between 9.9 m and 2 m.

  Does DDA create a false improvement mechanism? Yes, and precisely: (1) lower
  pressure ⇒ lower k·p^N₁ ⇒ higher leakage reward — genuine; (2) supplied demand
  unchanged ⇒ no service-shortfall signal — artifact; (3) with a binary
  violation term the brake is nearly inert. The reported result is a number like
  "73 % leakage reduction" of which, under PDA, a material share would be
  supply deficit rather than saving. This is exactly the kind of result that
  collapses under a post-defence PDA re-run.

  Aggravating inference (Medium-High): since Negm never declared a demand model
  and never changed EPANET's default, his 73.4 % was very likely measured in a 
  world where service shortfall is invisible. That elevates A7 from an internal
  check to a replication-correction of the nearest prior work — a standalone,
  publishable result that does not currently exist in the literature.

  C. Options.

  ┌─────────────────┬───────────┬────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
  │                 │ DDA only  │ PDA both   │ Hybrid (train DDA, eval PDA) │
  │                 │           │   phases   │                              │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Construct       │ Broken    │ ✅ intact  │ Partial                      │
  │ validity        │           │            │                              │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Reward hacking  │ Unblocked │ ✅ blocked │ Blocked at eval only         │
  │ F1              │           │            │                              │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Compute         │ Lowest    │ Medium     │ Medium                       │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Convergence     │ Surest    │ Good       │ Good                         │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Train/test      │ ✅        │ ✅         │ ❌ mismatch — policy learns  │
  │ consistency     │           │            │ a DDA artifact and collapses │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Risk            │ High      │ Low        │ High                         │
  └─────────────────┴───────────┴────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘

  Mandatory validation tests before any training (hard gate):

  ┌─────┬──────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐
  │  #  │               Test               │         Pass criterion         │
  ├─────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │ V1  │ Print the actual P₀, P_f, 1/e    │ Equal to intended values, not  │
  │     │ after configuration              │ the 0.07 m default             │
  ├─────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │ V2  │ Run an identical scenario under  │ Outputs must differ; identical │
  │     │ DDA and under PDA                │  output ⇒ PDA is not active    │
  ├─────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │ V3  │ Progressively lower all PRV      │ DSR must fall monotonically; a │
  │     │ settings and plot DSR            │  flat line ⇒ PDA inert         │
  ├─────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │     │ With P_f raised, check emitter   │ Leakage not curtailed; demand  │
  │ V4  │ flow still follows k·p^N₁ while  │ is curtailed — this resolves   │
  │     │ d < D                            │ G2                             │
  ├─────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │ V5  │ Mass balance: system input = Σd  │ Relative error < 10⁻⁴          │
  │     │ + ΣQ^leak                        │                                │
  └─────┴──────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘

  Design warning (retained from Phase 2, still applicable): if P_f is set equal
  to the violation threshold p^min, DSR and violation count become structurally 
  correlated and cease to be independent metrics. Recommendation: set P_f
  slightly above p^min to preserve partial independence, and declare the
  residual correlation in the interpretation.

  Recommendation: PDA for training and evaluation; DDA retained as a controlled
  ablation (A7), promoted to a standalone results subsection. DECISION: NOT 
  LOCKED — READY TO LOCK, awaiting researcher sign-off (B3).

  ---

  10. Leakage Model Decision Analysis

  A. FACT / EVIDENCE. Base relation Q_l = k P^n with n from 0.5 to 2.5 (Negm
  §2). FAVAD: fixed-area N₁=0.5, variable-area N₁=1.5, real networks between
  (May 1994; Lambert 2001). Real range 0.47–2.5 depending on soil, pipe
  properties and failure type (Walski et al. 2009 in Negm). Background/burst
  boundary: k < 0.196 background, k > 0.196 burst, in Ls⁻¹m⁻⁰·⁵ (García &
  Cabrera 2007 in Negm). Pipe material effect: plastic higher a_k, rigid lower
  (Giustolisi et al. 2008 in Hu). Negm, verbatim: "universal leakage exponent of
  1.18 which is the widely accepted value in literature (Araujo et al. 2006;
  Saldarriaga and Salcedo 2015b)", k_j proportional to adjacent pipe length,
  "kept under the background coefficient limit of 0.196", implemented via
  ENsetoption(4, emitter_exp); and "EPANET … uses the pressure dependent leakage
  equation (2-9) rather than the FAVAD equation … Essentially, EPANET
  introduces leaks by changing the emitter coefficient." Hu, verbatim: two
  end-node emitters, "confirmed to have sufficient accuracy, with an error below
  3.10 %"; "when a_k ≥ 1.5 the use of the emitter model is not recommended,
  which heavily affects the leakage calculation results"; a_k = 1.2; and b_k
  obtained "by forcing the total leakage to 10 % and 20 % of the total demand"
  (7.38e-9, 1.67e-8). Giustolisi et al. 2016: except where the leakage exponent
  equals 1, approximating pipe pressure by node pressure introduces error. WNTR 
  docs: discharge coefficient default 0.75 and adjustable, but "α is fixed at 
  0.5 … and cannot currently be changed by the user"; and "emitter coefficients
  are ignored by the WNTRSimulator."

  B. INFERENCE. (i) Option C is structurally disqualified: α locked at 0.5
  contradicts the literature's N₁ ≈ 1.0–1.2, and at α=0.5 pressure reduction has
  a much weaker effect on leakage — so the agent has less incentive to reduce
  pressure and the research result is biased downward. The distortion falls on
  precisely the mechanism the thesis studies. (ii) Without calibration to a
  target fraction of demand, "73 % leakage reduction" is uninterpretable because
  it scales with an arbitrary k — and Negm's 73.4 % came from an uncalibrated
  k. (iii) N₁ is the most sensitive parameter in the project: it sets how much
  leakage responds to pressure, hence the entire incentive structure of the
  agent.

  C. Options.

  Physical meaning
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: Q^leak_j = k_j p_j^{N₁} at nodes
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: d^leak_k = b_k L_k (P_{k,mean})^{a_k},
  half-flow to each end
  C: WNTR-native: d^leak = C_d A p^α √(2/ρ)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Pressure relation
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: Node pressure proxies pipe pressure
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: Mean pipe pressure — more accurate
  C: WNTR-native: α locked at 0.5
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Calibration
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: Single parameter k_j ∝ Σ L_ij
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: b_k forced to a % of demand
  C: WNTR-native: C_d, A
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Comparability
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: Negm
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: Hu (nearest joint pump+valve work)
  C: WNTR-native: None
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Implementation complexity
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: Low
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: Medium (~234 emitters on Net3)
  C: WNTR-native: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  N₁ sensitivity
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: ✅ free, but global only
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: ✅ free, ceiling 1.5
  C: WNTR-native: ❌ impossible
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  k sensitivity
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: ✅
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: ✅
  C: WNTR-native: Partial
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Generalisation effect
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: Neutral
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: Slightly better
  C: WNTR-native: Biased downward
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Reproducibility
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: High
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: High
  C: WNTR-native: High
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Verdict
  A: nodal emitter, global exponent: Primary
  B: pipe-level, two end emitters: Sensitivity
  C: WNTR-native: REJECTED

  Recommendation: Option A primary — Q^leak_j = k_j p_j^{1.18}, k_j ∝ Σ L_ij,
  k_j < 0.196 — plus Option B's calibration technique as mandatory: scale k_j so
  total leakage in the reference scenario equals 10 % and 20 % of total demand
  (two experimental levels, which also yields generalisation test L2a for free).
  Mandatory sensitivity S1 over N₁ ∈ {0.5, 1.0, 1.18, 1.2, 1.4} with a hard 1.5
  ceiling (Hu's constraint). Option B retained as a leakage-model sensitivity.
  Validation: verify computed Q^leak against EPANET's direct output; check
  monotonicity (lowering all PRV settings must lower total leakage); confirm the
  global N₁ was actually applied and not the 0.5 default. DECISION: NOT LOCKED 
  — READY TO LOCK (C1/C2/C3).

  ---

  11. State Decision Analysis

  A. FACT / EVIDENCE. Negm, verbatim: observations comprise "changes in leakage 
  rate after introducing a new PRV setting and the full list of PRV and VSP
  settings"; "the observation space describes the nodal leakage rate and
  actuator settings". Negm's own caveats: "this study depends on clean data …
  requires sensor data across the network" and "well-developed DRL models are
  very sensitive to incorrect observation data which could wrongly trigger
  destructive valve actions". Belfadil, verbatim: "at each time step, the agent
  receives the pressure at demand nodes, tank levels, and time; this is the
  state s of the environment." Hu MARL eq. (21): s = (s_demand1..N, s_tank1..Z);
  demand states "normalised between zero and one for each consumer node";
  s_tank,t=0 ∈ [0,1]. Mosetlhe: "the critical node is defined as the sensitive
  node with the lowest pressure"; control from critical-node pressure is
  established (Berardi 2015; Page 2017c). Olmuştur & Uysal 2025: "although
  maintaining constant pressure at a single critical point appears effective, it
  does not necessarily guarantee optimal pressure distribution across the whole
  system; therefore network-level modelling is essential."

  B. Feature-by-feature audit (the six questions applied):

  Feature: Pressure at instrumented nodes
  Physically observable?: ✅ SCADA
  Available at decision time?: ✅
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: Low
  Real-world available?: ✅
  Supports RQ?: ✅ core
  Verdict: INCLUDE
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Pressure at all nodes
  Physically observable?: ❌
  Available at decision time?: ✅
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: High (uses unavailable information)
  Real-world available?: ❌
  Supports RQ?: Partly
  Verdict: EXCLUDE
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Flow at metered sections
  Physically observable?: ✅ DMA inlet meters
  Available at decision time?: ✅
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: Low
  Real-world available?: ✅
  Supports RQ?: ✅
  Verdict: INCLUDE
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Tank level
  Physically observable?: ✅
  Available at decision time?: ✅
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: Low
  Real-world available?: ✅
  Supports RQ?: ✅ closes pump coupling
  Verdict: INCLUDE
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Actuator state (current setting)
  Physically observable?: ✅
  Available at decision time?: ✅
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: Low
  Real-world available?: ✅
  Supports RQ?: ✅
  Verdict: INCLUDE
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Previous action a_{t-1}
  Physically observable?: ✅
  Available at decision time?: ✅
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: Low
  Real-world available?: ✅
  Supports RQ?: ✅ required for the smoothness term
  Verdict: INCLUDE
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Time encoding sin/cos(2πt/24)
  Physically observable?: ✅
  Available at decision time?: ✅
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: 🔶 Yes — schedule memorisation
  Real-world available?: ✅
  Supports RQ?: Ambiguous
  Verdict: INCLUDE + test (F3)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Nodal leakage rate
  Physically observable?: ❌ a model quantity, not a measurement
  Available at decision time?: Only in simulation
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: Critical
  Real-world available?: ❌ does not exist
  Supports RQ?: Would inflate performance
  Verdict: PROHIBIT (oracle run only)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Total network leakage
  Physically observable?: 🔶 only via balance/MNF, with lag
  Available at decision time?: Lagged
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: Medium
  Real-world available?: Partly
  Supports RQ?: Partly
  Verdict: EXCLUDE from main
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: True demand multiplier
  Physically observable?: ❌
  Available at decision time?: ✅ in simulation
  Leaks future info?: YES — future information
  Shortcut risk?: Critical
  Real-world available?: ❌
  Supports RQ?: Destroys sub-question 1
  Verdict: PROHIBIT
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Feature: Recent history window (H steps)
  Physically observable?: ✅
  Available at decision time?: ✅
  Leaks future info?: No
  Shortcut risk?: Low
  Real-world available?: ✅
  Supports RQ?: Partly (POMDP aid)
  Verdict: OPTIONAL (LSTM alternative)

  C. Two information-leakage traps, stated precisely. (1) True demand multiplier
  in the observation ⇒ leakage of future information. If the agent sees the
  actual consumption multiplier for the current step, the uncertainty problem
  dissolves and sub-question 1 becomes vacuous. Permitted: the effect of demand
  (observed pressures, observed flows). Prohibited: the multiplier itself. (2)
  Time encoding is double-edged. With it, the agent can maintain an hourly
  lookup table instead of reacting to state — degenerating into a learned TMPC
  rather than adaptive control, which directly undercuts the Proposal's central
  claim. Remedy: keep it, but ablate it (A9) and run the pattern-break test
  (out-of-phase peak). If performance collapses, the policy was time-driven.

  D. Options. S1 minimal [p^CN, h^tank, a_{t-1}] — lowest dimension, most
  operational, comparable to industrial CLPC; but a single node does not
  represent network pressure distribution (Olmuştur & Uysal), so violations
  elsewhere may go unseen. S2 balanced [p^M, q^F, h^tank, a_{t-1}, φ(t)] — every
  component SCADA-measurable, controlled dimension, deployable, network
  coverage; the choice of M is itself a design decision needing documentation
  and sensitivity. S3 rich — adds all-node pressures, all pipe flows, nodal
  Q^leak, history; higher ceiling and valuable as an oracle upper bound, but not
  deployable and prone to sample-inefficiency and overfitting.

  Recommendation: S2 as the main design; S3 restricted to a single oracle run;
  S1 in ablation A8. The S2↔S3 gap quantifies the cost of observability — a
  quantity neither Negm nor Belfadil measured, and a genuine (if modest)
  contribution. Normalisation statistics computed from training scenarios only,
  stored and published. Validation: the pattern-break test; an audit that no
  feature exposes a future value; permutation importance to detect shortcut
  reliance. DECISION: NOT LOCKED — READY TO LOCK (F1/F2/F3/F4).

  ---

  12. Action Decision Analysis

  A. FACT / EVIDENCE. Hu MARL, verbatim: "The action of the pressure reducing 
  valve (PRV) agent was to set the outlet pressure, and its action space was
  determined based on the minimum pressure requirement of consumers and the
  valve inlet pressure. The valve inlet pressure of the PRV was calculated using
  1,000 random interaction episodes with the environment, and the maximum valve
  inlet pressure was set as the upper limit of the action space of the PRV
  agent." And: "The pump was modelled as a variable-speed pump with a speed 
  range of 0.7–1 considering mechanical constraints." Negm: action-space bounds
  "0 to 70 for valves and unlimited speed for pumps". Dai, Kim & Kim 2016 / Dai 
  2021, verbatim: "PRV models used so far for pressure regulation are two-state 
  models that cannot describe many situations occurring in WDS. In this paper we
  extend the existing model with a three-state model for the PRV … able to
  explain the three operating states of the PRV (open, normal, check valve)."
  Negm §7.2, verbatim: "it was assumed that pressure valves are able to process
  precise settings provided by the agent whereas in practice some control errors
  can be expected." Negm: "most water utilities change PRV settings daily."
  Proposal assumption: "تازیهجت یجیردت میظنت یارب هتسویپ لمع یاضف".

  B. INFERENCE — the physical problem no DRL work in the corpus models. When the
  agent commands an outlet-pressure setpoint, the PRV honours it only in the 
  normal state. If the setpoint exceeds available upstream head the valve goes
  fully open and the command is not realised; under reverse flow it becomes a
  check valve. Therefore commanded action ≠ realised action, part of the action
  space is effectively inert, and the reward signal contains plateau regions
  that slow learning and mislead interpretation. Also: Negm's "unlimited pump
  speed" is physically invalid; Hu's [0.7, 1.0] mechanical bound is
  scientifically better.

  C. PRV specifics. Setpoint interpretation: target outlet head in metres. Valid
  range: `[p^min_service, s̄_

● v], with s̄_v` derived empirically from 1,000 random rollouts (Hu's documented
  method — an empirical bound, not a guess). Operational-state behaviour: three
  states per Dai; only the normal state realises the command.

  D. VSP specifics. Speed ratio ω ∈ [0.7, 1.0], a mechanical constraint taken
  verbatim from Hu. Below 0.7 the pump is not a defensible operating point;
  above 1.0 exceeds nominal speed. Negm's "unlimited" is rejected on physical
  grounds.

  E. Options.

  ┌───────────────────┬────────────────┬──────────────────┬────────────────┐
  │                   │                │                  │ C: absolute +  │
  │                   │  A: absolute   │  B: incremental  │  rate-limit    │
  │                   │                │                  │   projection   │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │                   │ a_t =          │ s_t =            │ s_t =          │
  │ Formulation       │ [s^PRV_v, ω_u] │ clip(s_{t-1} +   │ clip(s̃_t,      │
  │                   │  directly      │ Δ_t, …)          │ s_{t-1} ± δ)   │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Cumulative drift  │ None           │ Possible         │ None           │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │                   │                │ Requires s_{t-1} │                │
  │ Markovianity      │ Better         │  in state        │ Better         │
  │                   │                │ (present in S2)  │                │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Physical          │ ❌ large jumps │                  │ ✅ hard        │
  │ feasibility       │  possible      │ ✅ structural    │ guarantee      │
  │ between steps     │                │                  │                │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Comparability     │ ✅ they emit   │                  │                │
  │ with optimiser    │ settings       │ ❌ complicated   │ ✅             │
  │ baselines         │ directly       │                  │                │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │                   │ Negm,          │                  │                │
  │ Corpus precedent  │ Belfadil, Hu,  │ None             │ —              │
  │                   │ Mosetlhe — all │                  │                │
  │                   │  absolute      │                  │                │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Smoothness        │ Penalty only   │ Structural       │ Structural     │
  │ handling          │                │                  │ plus penalty   │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ New parameter     │ —              │ δ                │ δ ⇒            │
  │                   │                │                  │ sensitivity S8 │
  ├───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Risk              │ Medium         │ Medium           │ Low            │
  └───────────────────┴────────────────┴──────────────────┴────────────────┘

  F. Action Realization Rate — definition and validation. Definition: at every
  step the realised setting is read back from EPANET after the hydraulic solve;
  ARR = (steps in which the commanded setpoint was actually realised) / (total 
  steps), reported per actuator and aggregated. Validation: construct a scenario
  with a setpoint deliberately above available upstream head — ARR must drop
  measurably; if it does not, the read-back is wrong, not the physics. Why it 
  matters: it is the cheapest methodological contribution available (one logged
  quantity), it has no precedent in the corpus, it directly tests operational
  executability, and it diagnoses the reward plateaus that the three-state PRV
  behaviour creates.

  Recommendation: Option C. s^PRV_{v,t} = clip(s̃_{v,t}, s^PRV_{v,t-1} ± δ_v) and
  ω_{u,t} = clip(ω̃_{u,t}, ω_{u,t-1} ± δ_u), with s^PRV_v ∈ [p^min_service, s̄_v]
  and ω_u ∈ [0.7, 1.0]; policy outputs tanh, rescaled to the physical range, so
  structurally invalid actions are impossible. δ at 10 % of range as reference,
  {5 %, 10 %, 20 %} in sensitivity S8. Pure incremental in ablation A11. ARR
  logged and reported. Do not assume a mathematically valid action is physically
  executable — ARR is the instrument that enforces this. DECISION: NOT LOCKED —
  READY TO LOCK (G1/G2/G3).

  ---

  13. Reward Decision Analysis

  A. FACT / EVIDENCE. Negm eq. 4-3: R = Σ_j[(v_after − v_before)·scale₁ + ((Q_lb
  − Q_la)/Q_lb)·scale₂], with v ∈ {0,1} indicating whether node pressure is
  below 10 m or above 70 m. Negm §4.2.4: ratio 6:1 best for leakage, 1:5 best
  for violations, 3:1 best overall; and "the two extremes of the ratios do not
  necessarily produce the best result for the corresponding objective". Negm
  Table 5-4 + p. 130: PPO reached 73.4 % leakage with 304 violations and
  "exploited the leakage objective at the expense of the pressure violation 
  objective". Ledesma 2020 and 2024: the four canonical WDN control objectives
  are economic, safety, smoothness, water quality. Ma et al. 2024: frequent pump
  switching caused "wear of pump units"; the proposed scheme "keeps the number
  of pumps relatively stable". Olmuştur & Uysal 2025: PID on PRVs is prone to
  "instability or oscillation". Hu MARL: "water loss dominates the scheduling 
  process, and the scheduling solutions … are mainly affected by the demand 
  pattern rather than the energy tariff"; pump efficiency "simplified to 0.75";
  eq. (17) H_tank,fin ≥ H_tank,ini as a hard constraint.

  B. INFERENCE — two structural defects in Negm's form, plus one omission.

  Defect: W1: binary violation term
  Analysis: v ∈ {0,1} ⇒ zero gradient between 9.9 m and 2 m. The agent is
  indifferent between a marginal violation and a catastrophic outage
  Correction: Continuous severity ν_{j,t} in metres ⇒ meaningful gradient at
  every level
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Defect: W2: per-node normalisation
  Analysis: (Q_lb − Q_la)/Q_lb gives a node with negligible baseline leakage the

  same weight as a node with enormous leakage ⇒ the agent is incentivised to
  work on unimportant nodes
  Correction: Normalise by total network leakage in the reference scenario
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Defect: W3: only two terms
  Analysis: Negm has leakage + violation. The Proposal requires four objectives:

  leakage, pressure, smoothness, energy
  Correction: Terms three and four designed from the outset with literature
  backing (Ledesma, Ma)

  C. The reward-hacking evidence, and the confound. Direct evidence: PPO with
  Negm's reward achieved the best leakage and the worst violations, in Negm's
  own words "exploited". New inference this phase (Medium-High): Table 5-4
  partitions cleanly — the ~65 %/46–78-violation cluster is exactly the tuned
  agents plus the classical optimisers, and the ~73 %/264–304 cluster is exactly
  the untuned on-policy agents; Negm states "All untuned DRL algorithms
  performed less favourably". So the hacking magnitude is entangled with (a) the
  binary violation term, (b) absent tuning, (c) the 20 k budget. Consequence: 
  the project cannot claim its reward redesign fixed the behaviour unless A6 
  runs at equal tuning and equal budget. This is why A6 is promoted to Tier 1
  and why the contribution wording in N1/A3 must stay conservative.

  D. Failure modes F1–F11 — resolution ownership.

  #: F1
  Failure mode: Faking leakage reduction via supply curtailment
  Resolved by: Simulator choice (PDA) + evaluation (DSR as an independent
  primary
  metric)
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F2
  Failure mode: Indifference to violation severity
  Resolved by: Reward design (continuous ν in metres, β=2) + evaluation (max
  violation, P95)
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F3
  Failure mode: Weight choice silently favouring leakage
  Resolved by: Evaluation (full Pareto front) + feasibility-first rule
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F4
  Failure mode: Per-node normalisation distortion
  Resolved by: Reward design (global normalisation by L^ref)
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F5
  Failure mode: Focusing only on night hours (highest leakage)
  Resolved by: Evaluation (hour-resolved reporting) — Negm found a 3–6 am
  vulnerability
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F6
  Failure mode: Excessive conservatism (high w_V ⇒ high pressure, high leakage)
  Resolved by: Evaluation (both metrics always reported; comparison against the
  fixed-pressure baseline, which must not be beaten in the wrong direction) +
  test T10
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F7
  Failure mode: Gaming the energy term by draining tanks
  Resolved by: Reward design (tank-recovery constraint) — now evidence-backed
  via
  Hu eq. (17)
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F8
  Failure mode: Frozen policy from an over-weighted smoothness term
  Resolved by: Ablation (A3, w_S=0) + reporting setting variance; Negm: "if the
  agent shows no change in PRV settings it has probably extracted a local
  minimum"
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F9
  Failure mode: Time-driven rather than state-driven policy
  Resolved by: Evaluation (pattern-break OOD test) + ablation A9
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F10
  Failure mode: Overfitting to the demand seed
  Resolved by: Experimental design (three separate seed streams; held-out test
  stream)
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: F11
  Failure mode: Unrealisable commands (fully-open / check-valve regions)
  Resolved by: Evaluation (ARR, read-back of realised settings)
  Status: ✅ Resolved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: NEW
  Failure mode: Attribution confound — cannot separate reward redesign from
  tuning and budget
  Resolved by: Ablation A6 at equal tuning and budget
  Status: 🔶 Resolved only if A6 runs

  E. Options.

  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Implementation     │ Low–medium        │ High           │ Very high    │
  │ complexity         │                   │                │              │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Corpus precedent   │ ✅ Negm           │ 🔶 inference   │ ✅ Ledesma   │
  │ in WDN             │                   │ only (G9)      │ 2024         │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Scope              │ None — approved   │ 🔶 borderline  │ 🔶 expanding │
  │                    │ innovation 2      │                │              │
  └────────────────────┴───────────────────┴────────────────┴──────────────┘

  Recommended form:

  r_t = −[ w_L·L̃_t + w_V·Ṽ_t + w_S·S̃_t + w_E·Ẽ_t ], all terms dimensionless and
  pre-normalised against a reference scenario:

  L̃_t = Σ_j Q^leak_{j,t} / L^ref_step · Ṽ_t = (1/|J|) Σ_j (ν_{j,t}/ν^scale)^β
  with ν_{j,t} = max(0, p^min − p_{j,t}) + max(0, p_{j,t} − p^max) in metres ·
  S̃_t = (1/(|V|+|U|))[ Σ_v (|Δs_{v,t}|/δ_v)² + Σ_u (|Δω_{u,t}|/δ_u)² ] · Ẽ_t = 
  P^pump_t Δt / E^ref_step with η = 0.75 and no tariff modelling · plus the hard
  constraint H_tank,fin ≥ H_tank,ini.

  Weight selection: sweep w_V/w_L ∈ {1/7, 1/5, 1/3, 1, 3, 5, 7} at identical
  budget and seeds, report the entire Pareto front, select by feasibility-first.
  Final weights are NOT chosen here and must not be — that is an explicit 
  researcher decision after the front exists.

  DECISION: NOT LOCKED — READY TO LOCK for the form (H1); weights explicitly 
  deferred (H5); β reference + mandatory A6 (H2); Option B → Tier 3 (H3); Option
  C → future work (H4).

  ---

  14. Reward Validation — battery T1–T10

  The reward must be validated on synthetic inputs, before any training, so that
  training results are interpretable. Each test constructs a controlled state
  transition and checks the sign and magnitude of the reward.

  #: T1
  Scenario: Leakage falls, all pressures in range
  Expected reward direction: Increase
  Status: MANDATORY — sanity floor
  Questionable assumption?: None
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T2
  Scenario: Leakage constant, one node drops to 5 m
  Expected reward direction: Decrease, appreciably
  Status: MANDATORY
  Questionable assumption?: None
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T3
  Scenario: Leakage −10 %, but one node violates by 3 m
  Expected reward direction: Must decrease (feasibility-first). If it increases,

  the weights are wrong
  Status: MANDATORY — decisive
  Questionable assumption?: ⚠️ Depends on the chosen w_V/w_L; the test defines
  what "wrong" means only after a weight is picked, so it must be re-run for
  every swept weight
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T4
  Scenario: Peak demand, multiplier ×1.5
  Expected reward direction: Must not become artificially generous;
  normalisation
  should absorb the scale
  Status: MANDATORY
  Questionable assumption?: ⚠️ Assumes L^ref is scale-appropriate; if the
  reference scenario is itself a peak scenario the test is weak
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T5
  Scenario: Night, multiplier ×0.4, high pressure
  Expected reward direction: Should signal the leakage-reduction opportunity
  Status: RECOMMENDED
  Questionable assumption?: None
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T6
  Scenario: All nodes at p^max
  Expected reward direction: The violation term must activate (upper bound, not
  only lower)
  Status: MANDATORY
  Questionable assumption?: None — this specifically catches a one-sided
  violation implementation
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T7
  Scenario: One PRV closed as far as possible
  Expected reward direction: DSR must fall and reward must go negative. If not,
  PDA is not working
  Status: MANDATORY — decisive
  Questionable assumption?: None. This is the strongest single check that the
  PDA/emitter separation is real
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T8
  Scenario: ω = 0.7 forced at peak
  Expected reward direction: Reward must register the violation caused by the
  pressure drop
  Status: RECOMMENDED
  Questionable assumption?: None
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T9
  Scenario: Compute L̃, Ṽ, S̃, Ẽ in the reference scenario
  Expected reward direction: All must be O(1); if one is ~10³ larger,
  normalisation is broken
  Status: MANDATORY
  Questionable assumption?: None. This is the guard against the β=2/ν^scale
  miscalibration risk in H1
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: T10
  Scenario: Reward of the fixed-pressure baseline policy
  Expected reward direction: Yields a reference number; any learned policy must
  beat it or training failed
  Status: MANDATORY
  Questionable assumption?: None

  Mandatory: T1, T2, T3, T4, T6, T7, T9, T10. Optional: T5, T8. Hard gate: T3 
  and T7 must pass before any training run. If T7 fails, every downstream result
  is invalid because the PDA mechanism is inert.

  Methodological note. This battery does automatically, before training, what
  Negm did visually after training via five render functions ("the render
  function … can help in diagnosing any problem in the agent behaviour. It can
  also help in fine-tuning the reward function"). Automated pre-training tests
  are cheaper and repeatable.

  ---

  15. Demand Uncertainty Decision Analysis

  A. FACT / EVIDENCE. Hu E-PPO, verbatim: two-step randomisation "to mimic the
  time and space effects" — hourly random multipliers on the default pattern,
  then nodal random multipliers on base demand; "Both random multipliers of time
  and space follow the truncated normal distribution … restricted to a certain
  range of values, such as (1 − Δ, 1 + Δ)"; "The initial water levels of the 
  tanks follow a uniform distribution of (0, 1)"; large consumers — "… compared
  to the general consumer and do not apply a randomisation method to it". Hu
  MARL: multipliers "follow a truncated normal distribution with a range from
  0.7 to 1.3" (Δ = 0.3); "All the test cases are randomly generated from the 
  same distribution as the training cases." Hu E-PPO evaluation: "100 random 
  test cases from the same distribution as the training cases", plus "15 
  optimisation test cases under three different demand distributions". Hu E-PPO,
  verbatim: "when the environment has great uncertainty, it is difficult to 
  learn an optimal strategy network, as the state transition becomes blurred.
  The selection of the agent tends to be conservative." Proposal: only "طیارش
   and the Proposal itself criticises ;"فرصمم‌ک طیارش و فرصم جوا تاعاس ،یداع فرصم
  prior work for examining performance "زا فرصم یاهوگلا و تباث اًتبسن طیارش رد
  ."هدشن‌ییعت شیپ

  B. INFERENCE. The Proposal's three named scenarios are deterministic 
  scenarios, not uncertainty — they are precisely the deficiency the Proposal
  attributes to prior work. Hu's protocol has three properties the Proposal
  lacks: uncertainty that is both spatial and temporal, a parametric and 
  reproducible distribution, and a physical refinement (large consumers carry
  lower uncertainty). And because both Hu papers tested in-distribution only,
  holding out a Δ level is a genuine advance rather than a replication.

  C. Options.

  Definition
  A: scalar perturbation: One multiplier × base pattern
  B: Hu-style stochastic: Spatial-temporal truncated normal, Δ parametric
  C: historical profiles: Real SCADA data
  D: Monte Carlo: Mass sampling
  E: distribution shift: Test on unseen Δ or pattern shape
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Realism
  A: scalar perturbation: Low
  B: Hu-style stochastic: High
  C: historical profiles: Highest
  D: Monte Carlo: High
  E: distribution shift: High
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Reproducibility
  A: scalar perturbation: Highest
  B: Hu-style stochastic: High (seed + parameter)
  C: historical profiles: Low (proprietary)
  D: Monte Carlo: High
  E: distribution shift: High
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Difficulty
  A: scalar perturbation: Negligible
  B: Hu-style stochastic: Low
  C: historical profiles: High (data access)
  D: Monte Carlo: Medium
  E: distribution shift: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Generalisation value
  A: scalar perturbation: Low
  B: Hu-style stochastic: High
  C: historical profiles: High
  D: Monte Carlo: High
  E: distribution shift: Highest
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Coverage
  A: scalar perturbation: One-dimensional
  B: Hu-style stochastic: Demand space
  C: historical profiles: Limited history
  D: Monte Carlo: Wide
  E: distribution shift: Out-of-distribution
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Cost
  A: scalar perturbation: Negligible
  B: Hu-style stochastic: Low
  C: historical profiles: Low after data
  D: Monte Carlo: High
  E: distribution shift: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Evidence
  A: scalar perturbation: Mosetlhe (120 % demand test)
  B: Hu-style stochastic: Hu ×2
  C: historical profiles: — (absent from corpus)
  D: Monte Carlo: —
  E: distribution shift: Negm (random bursts)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Verdict
  A: scalar perturbation: OPTIONAL (cheap stress test)
  B: Hu-style stochastic: MANDATORY
  C: historical profiles: SCOPE-EXPANDING → exclude
  D: Monte Carlo: OPTIONAL → exclude on cost
  E: distribution shift: MANDATORY

  D. Train / validation / test separation — engineered, not assumed.

  ┌────────────────┬─────────────┬─────────────────┬───────────────────────┐
  │                │             │   Validation    │                       │
  │   Dimension    │    Train    │   (weight &     │  Test (once, at the   │
  │                │             │   checkpoint    │         end)          │
  │                │             │   selection)    │                       │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Seed stream    │ {1..S_tr}   │ {S_tr+1..}      │ Separate and locked   │
  │                │             │                 │ until the end         │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │                │             │                 │ same                  │
  │ Δ              │ {0.3, 0.6}  │ same            │ (in-distribution) + Δ │
  │                │             │                 │  = 0.9 held out (OOD) │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Daily pattern  │ Net3        │ same            │ same + out-of-phase   │
  │ shape          │ reference   │                 │ pattern (OOD #2)      │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Initial tank   │ U(0,1)      │ U(0,1)          │ U(0,1) with a         │
  │ level          │             │                 │ separate seed         │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Normalisation  │ computed    │                 │ applied, never        │
  │ statistics     │ from train  │ applied         │ recomputed            │
  │                │ only        │                 │                       │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Episode count  │ —           │ ≥50             │ ≥100 per seed         │
  └────────────────┴─────────────┴─────────────────┴───────────────────────┘

  Hard rules: reward-weight and checkpoint selection only on validation, never
  on test; the test set runs once, at the end; if the design changes after
  seeing test results, a new test set must be constructed and the change
  reported explicitly.

  Risk requiring a Step-2 check: if Δ = 0.9 is so severe that even the best
  baseline violates constraints, the performance ceiling is artificial. Check in
  Step 2; if so, reduce the OOD Δ to ≈0.75 and report the adjustment.

  Recommendation: B mandatory as generator + E mandatory as the evaluation layer
  + A optional as a cheap stress test + C excluded (scope) + D excluded (cost).
  DECISION: NOT LOCKED — READY TO LOCK (K1/K2/K3/K4).

  ---

  16. Baseline Decision Analysis

  A. FACT / EVIDENCE. Seminar lists FOPC (fixed outlet pressure control) and
  TMPC (time-modulated) among pressure-management strategies, and reports
  Vicente et al. 2016 calling fixed-outlet control effectively obsolete while
  remaining the industrial reference — and Vicente et al. 2016 is now fully 
  identified (§7.1). Proposal: "نکشراشف یاهریش تباث لرتنک اب …هسیاقم". Negm:
  "most water utilities change PRV settings daily" ⇒ TMPC is industry practice,
  not a straw man. Negm Table 5-4 (decisive): DE achieved the best average 
  reward (995.2) and the fewest violations (46) — better than all eight DRL 
  agents. Hu: "GA converges after 100 epochs … therefore, the results of GA are
  considered as optimal solutions and benchmarks". Mosetlhe: classical P/PID
  control from one measured point is standard practice. Olmuştur & Uysal 2025:
  "one of the most widely used methods for real-time PRV adjustment is the PID
  controller", with 19 % loss reduction and 41 % fewer failures in a real field 
  deployment. Seminar on MPC: "تسا هتسباو یضایر لدم تحص هب اًلماک نآ درکلمع تقد".
  Proposal sub-question 2 ⇒ a single-objective control is required. Kåge 2025:
  "only a few studies have benchmarked policy-based against value-based RL
  algorithms".

  B. INFERENCE — the fairness question is where a comparison is won or lost. Two
  structural asymmetries: DRL trains once then infers in 0.42 s, whereas
  metaheuristics must solve each case separately at 1181–1699 s (Hu Table 3).
  And regime dependence: Negm ch. 5 (background leakage, quasi-static) had
  metaheuristics beating DRL, while ch. 6 (random bursts, dynamic) had DRL
  winning. Therefore the test regime determines the answer. If the thesis tests
  only quasi-static scenarios it will likely report DRL losing; if only dynamic,
  winning. The honest design tests both regimes and reports the dependence as a
  finding.

  C. Classification.

  Baseline: Fixed-outlet PRV
  Class: MANDATORY
  What it isolates: Absolute reference — without it no number means anything
  Fairness requirements: Tuned by an explicit industrial criterion: the lowest
  setting satisfying p^min at peak hour. Not arbitrary — an arbitrary setting
  manufactures a weak baseline
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: TMPC
  Class: MANDATORY
  What it isolates: Is the advantage from learning, or merely from changing 
  settings hourly? The single most important baseline for innovation 1
  Fairness requirements: Same network, same test scenarios, same leakage model,
  same PDA
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: DE
  Class: MANDATORY
  What it isolates: Performance ceiling — how far from optimal are we?
  Fairness requirements: Hydraulic-evaluation budget documented and increased to
   
  plateau. Giving DE 50 evaluations when it needs 5,000 builds a straw baseline
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: Single-objective (leakage-only) PPO
  Class: MANDATORY
  What it isolates: Direct control for sub-question 2
  Fairness requirements: Identical budget, seeds, test set
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: SAC
  Class: MANDATORY (Phase 2 said "recommended"; promoted — internal validity
  requires it)
  What it isolates: Is the advantage from PPO or from the environment/reward?
  Fairness requirements: Equal timestep budget, equal seed count, equal tuning 
  trials
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: P / PID on the critical node
  Class: RECOMMENDED
  What it isolates: Is RL needed at all, or does simple feedback suffice? Strong

  field evidence (Olmuştur & Uysal)
  Fairness requirements: Same network and test set
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: GA
  Class: RECOMMENDED
  What it isolates: Most conventional in both international and Persian PM
  literature; Hu's optimal reference
  Fairness requirements: Documented evaluation budget
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: PSO
  Class: OPTIONAL
  What it isolates: Metaheuristic diversity; weaker than DE in both Negm and Hu
  Fairness requirements: —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: Nelder-Mead
  Class: OPTIONAL
  What it isolates: Cheap local-optimisation reference
  Fairness requirements: —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: MPC
  Class: REJECTED
  What it isolates: High cost, marginal value in this scope; a P-controller
  answers the same question far cheaper
  Fairness requirements: —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Baseline: MADDPG
  Class: REJECTED
  What it isolates: Scope change; approved algorithm is PPO
  Fairness requirements: —

  Uniform fairness protocol across all baselines: same network, same demand
  scenarios (same seeds), same simulator, same leakage model, same PDA
  parameters, same constraints, same locked test set. Report the four cost
  quantities separately (§4.5 M3) plus the break-even ≈ 4 operational cases.

  Pre-accepted risk: DE may beat PPO in the quasi-static regime. This must be 
  reported honestly and interpreted as regime dependence — a finding, not a 
  failure. Do not confuse "lower leakage" with "better system performance", and 
  do not confuse "PPO is chosen" with "PPO is proven superior".

  DECISION: NOT LOCKED — READY TO LOCK (M1/M2/M3).

  ---

  17. Evaluation Decision Analysis

  Governing rule, derived from evidence rather than caution: Negm Table 5-4
  shows any leakage figure can be bought by paying in violations. Therefore 
  every leakage report must carry violations and DSR in the same row of the same
  table.

  Primary metrics (decisive in ranking).

  Metric: Leakage volume
  Formula: L = Σ_t Δt Σ_j k_j p_{j,t}^{N₁}
  Construct measured: The core objective, m³/day; reported absolute and relative
   
  to the fixed baseline
  Why needed: The thesis subject
  Gameable?: Yes — via service curtailment, hence never reported alone
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Metric: Violation volume
  Formula: VV = Σ_t Σ_j ν_{j,t} Δt (metre·hours)
  Construct measured: Total constraint-breach severity
  Why needed: Captures severity, unlike a count
  Gameable?: Hard to game; insensitive to distribution
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Metric: Violation count
  Formula: #{(j,t) : ν_{j,t} > 0}
  Construct measured: Breach frequency
  Why needed: Comparability with Negm, who reported exactly this (PPO 304, DE
  46)
  Gameable?: Yes — by trading many small for few large, hence paired with max
  violation
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Metric: Maximum violation
  Formula: max_{j,t} ν_{j,t}
  Construct measured: Worst-case safety
  Why needed: An 8 m breach is unacceptable even with a good mean
  Gameable?: No — cannot be averaged away
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Metric: DSR
  Formula: Σ_{t,j} d_{j,t} / Σ_{t,j} D_{j,t}
  Construct measured: Service adequacy
  Why needed: The only detector of failure mode F1. Meaningful only under PDA
  Gameable?: No

  Secondary metrics. Mean network pressure p̄ (context, not ranked). Pressure
  variability sd_t(p̄_t) or max_t p̄_t − min_t p̄_t — operationalises the 
  Proposal's otherwise undefined "pressure stability". Pump energy E = Σ_t 
  P^pump_t Δt (kWh). Actuator movement Σ_t Σ_v |Δs_{v,t}| + Σ_t Σ_u |Δω_{u,t}|.
  Tank recovery |h^tank_T − h^tank_0| (enforces F7; evidence-backed via Hu eq.
  17). ARR (operational validity). Inference time per decision step (Proposal
  objective 3). Training time (reported separately, once).

  Learning diagnostics. Episodic return; value loss; policy loss; entropy
  (detects policy collapse); explained variance; clip fraction and approx-KL
  (PPO-specific health); learning curves as mean ± SD across seeds — reported so
  a reader can see whether each algorithm converged or merely ran out of
  budget.

  Ranking rule — pre-registration mandatory (N2). max violation > safety 
  threshold ⇒ FAIL, unranked · DSR < 0.99 ⇒ FAIL · among feasible policies rank
  by leakage L · on statistical ties, by energy, then actuator movement. In 
  parallel plot the leakage↔violation-volume Pareto front for every policy — the
  lexicographic rule yields one number; the front is the scientific answer to
  sub-question 2. Safety threshold derived from the best baseline in Step 2, not
  chosen arbitrarily, and that derivation reported.

  Excluded: CO₂ — collinear with leakage via a fixed 176.7 kgCO₂/ML factor, and
  that factor is UK-specific (H7).

  Hour-resolved reporting mandatory — closes F5; Negm located a 3–6 am
  vulnerability.

  DECISION: NOT LOCKED — READY TO LOCK (N1/N2/N3/N4).

  ---

  18. Ablation and Sensitivity Decision Analysis

  Ablations. Purpose: answer "which component produced the improvement?" Without
  it, no contribution claim is attributable.

  #: A14
  Ablation: PRV-only vs PRV+Pump
  RQ answered: How much does adding the pump contribute? → innovation 1
  Cost: 1 arm
  Value: Critical
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: YES
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A5
  Ablation: Single- vs multi-objective reward
  RQ answered: Sub-question 2 / innovation 2
  Cost: 1 arm
  Value: Critical
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: YES
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A12
  Ablation: Trained without vs with uncertainty
  RQ answered: Is uncertainty-training necessary? → innovation 3
  Cost: 1 arm
  Value: Critical
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: YES
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A7
  Ablation: DDA vs PDA
  RQ answered: Magnitude of the DDA artifact — construct validity, and a
  standalone result
  Cost: 1 arm
  Value: Critical
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: YES — own subsection
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A6
  Ablation: Binary vs β=1 vs β=2
  RQ answered: Isolates the reward's contribution from tuning and budget
  Cost: 3 arms
  Value: Critical (promoted)
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: YES
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A8
  Ablation: State S1 / S2 / S3
  RQ answered: Cost of observability
  Cost: 3 arms
  Value: High
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: Recommended
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A9

  RQ answered: Tests F9 (time-driven policy)
  Cost: 1 arm
  Value: High
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: Recommended
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A13
  Ablation: PPO vs SAC
  RQ answered: Algorithm effect
  Cost: (= baseline)
  Value: High
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: Recommended
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A11
  Ablation: Absolute vs incremental
  RQ answered: Is structural smoothing better than a penalty?
  Cost: 1 arm
  Value: Medium
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: Optional
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A1–A4
  Ablation: Remove w_L / w_V / w_S / w_E individually
  RQ answered: Sanity checks. A2 (w_V=0) is the upper bound of reward hacking — 
  report the number so the reader sees why the constraint is needed
  Cost: 4 arms
  Value: High (A2), Medium (rest)
  Budget: Reduced acceptable
  Thesis core?: Recommended
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: A10
  Ablation: PPO vs Recurrent PPO
  RQ answered: POMDP hypothesis. A negative result is itself useful against a
  literature that adds LSTMs by default
  Cost: 1 arm
  Value: Medium
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: Optional
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: B-arm
  Ablation: Constrained formulation
  RQ answered: Stronger answer to sub-question 2
  Cost: 1 family
  Value: Medium
  Budget: Full
  Thesis core?: Optional (first cut)

  Fairness rule across every tier: identical budget, identical seeds, identical 
  test set. Unequal budget converts an ablation into an artifact — the exact
  error documented in the reference work.

  Sensitivities.

  #: S1
  Parameter: N₁
  Range: 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.18 / 1.2 / 1.4, hard ceiling 1.5
  Why sensitive: The most sensitive parameter in the project — sets how strongly

  pressure reduction cuts leakage, hence the agent's entire incentive
  Priority: MANDATORY
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S2
  Parameter: Reference leakage level
  Range: total leakage = 10 % / 20 % of demand
  Why sensitive: Every reported percentage depends on it
  Priority: MANDATORY
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S3
  Parameter: p^min
  Range: 10 / 14 / 20 / 25 m
  Why sensitive: Defines what counts as a violation ⇒ the whole ranking. Also 
  the
   bypass for G7
  Priority: MANDATORY
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S6
  Parameter: w_V/w_L
  Range: 1/7 … 7/1
  Why sensitive: Moves the trade-off — this is the weight sweep
  Priority: MANDATORY
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S9
  Parameter: PRV placement
  Range: P-GA vs P-OP sets
  Why sensitive: Negm: performance "strongly dependent on valve location and
  coverage"; and P-GA carries a co-optimisation bias
  Priority: MANDATORY (promoted)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S5
  Parameter: P_f
  Range: 15 / 20 / 25 m
  Why sensitive: The 0.07 m default silently disables PDA
  Priority: Covered by V1–V3
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S7
  Parameter: Δ
  Range: 0.3 / 0.6 / 0.9
  Why sensitive: Sets problem difficulty
  Priority: Covered by A12
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S10
  Parameter: PPO hyperparameters
  Range: limited sweep
  Why sensitive: Negm: tuning "significantly improves performance"
  Priority: Covered by the declared tuning budget
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S4
  Parameter: p^max
  Range: 70 / 100 / 200 m
  Why sensitive: Network-dependent (Negm used 70 on J&X, 200 on SZ08)
  Priority: Optional
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: S8
  Parameter: δ_v, δ_u
  Range: 5 % / 10 % / 20 %
  Why sensitive: A rate limit can silently cap performance
  Priority: Optional

  Method: one-factor-at-a-time around the reference point, at 3 seeds rather
  than 5 (a declared cost reduction), reported as a tornado plot so effect
  magnitudes are directly comparable.

  DECISION: NOT LOCKED — READY TO LOCK (P1/P2/P3 and the sensitivity set).

  19. Reproducibility Decision Analysis

  Traceability chain (Q3):

  git SHA
   → config YAML  (network, N₁, leakage level, PDA params, weights, β, action
  bounds, δ, Δ)
     → seed triple  (init/action · demand-train · demand-test)
       → run directory
         → eval_episodes.parquet   (every metric, every episode, raw)
           → metrics_summary.json
             → versioned figure/table script
               → figure or table in the thesis (run ID in caption or appendix)
                 → interpretive sentence in the text

  Run directory contents: config.snapshot.yaml, git_commit.txt (SHA +
  dirty/clean state), env_freeze.txt, seeds.json, model_final.zip,
  model_best.zip, train_log.csv, eval_episodes.parquet, metrics_summary.json,
  hydraulic_validation.json (V1–V5 results).

  Experiment ID: <phase>-<network>-<algo>-<reward>-<demand>-<seed>, e.g.
  E2-net3-ppo-mo3to1-d06-s01.

  Hard rule: every figure and table in the thesis carries its run ID. Anything 
  that cannot be traced to a specific run does not go in the thesis.

  Environment (Q1): Python 3.12 in a dedicated .venv; requirements.txt with
  exact pins (==, never >=) plus pip freeze output; EPANET 2.2; WNTR ≥1.0;
  Stable-Baselines3 2.x; sb3-contrib 2.x (only if the constrained arm runs);
  Gymnasium ≥0.29; PyTorch pinned compatible with SB3; NumPy, SciPy, Pandas,
  Matplotlib all pinned; Optuna optional for tuning. Hardware and CPU/GPU 
  recorded — Hu reported an RTX 3070 + i7-11700K, Negm a CPU-only Ryzen 4700U,
  which is exactly why their wall-clock figures are not mutually comparable.
  Note: EPyT (Kyriakou et al. 2023) is a viable alternative EPANET-Python 
  binding — used by Cyriac — but WNTR is chosen because it matches Hu's 
  environment.

  Determinism (Q2): three separate, documented seed streams — (i) network
  initialisation and action sampling, (ii) demand-scenario generation for
  train/validation, (iii) demand-scenario generation for test, never used in 
  training. Exact seeds recorded per run.
  torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True) where possible; where impossible, 
  declare it and claim statistical rather than bit-exact reproducibility. EPANET
  itself is deterministic, so these three streams are the only stochastic
  sources — an advantage to be exploited.

  Evidence protection (Q4): the modified Net3 .inp — recording every
  modification (added valves, deleted pump control rules, closed-pipe status) —
  version-controlled in git; historical results never overwritten, each re-run
  gets a new directory; branch for meaningful changes; known-good baselines
  preserved.

  Nothing was installed. DECISION: NOT LOCKED — READY TO LOCK (Q1/Q2/Q3/Q4).

  ▎ results/, src/ all empty). So "previously invalidated outputs" is empty

  21. Novelty Consistency Check

  ▎ Conservative language throughout. Where a contribution is only a combination
  ▎ of known components, that is stated. Where novelty depends on the 
  ▎ experimental protocol, that is stated.

  #: 1
  Contribution Claim: Coordinated PRV + VSP control with a single-agent 
  continuous PPO
  Closest Prior Work: Negm 2024 §7.3 (explicitly proposes it as future work);
  Hu,
   Gao & Zhong 2023 (does it with MADDPG on Net3 under demand uncertainty);
  Monsef et al. 2017 (PRV+VSP with DE since 2017); Li et al. 2024 preprint (PRV
   + fixed/variable-speed pumps via GA)
  Evidence: Negm §7.3 verbatim: "Incorporating other pressure influencing
  network
  elements such as pumps … add the new elements to the action space controlled
  by a single agent" — i.e. our innovation 1 is his published future-work item
  Remaining Difference: Single-agent on-policy PPO versus multi-agent off-policy

  MADDPG; and quantified attribution of the pump's  contribution via A14, which
   none of them isolated
  Strength: Weak as "what is controlled"; moderate as "quantified attribution"
  Confidence: High for the overlap; Medium for the sufficiency of the reframing
  Risk: 🔴 High — "implementing a thesis's future work" is a thin contribution
  on
  its own
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Contribution Claim: Multi-objective reward design
  Closest Prior Work: Negm 2024 (weighted multi-objective with a 3-D weight
  trade-off study); Kåge et al. 2025 (multi-objective reward design is routine
  practice; "automating reward function design" is the open gap); Joo, Jeong & 
  Kang 2024 (pump-switching penalty inside a DRL reward)
  Evidence: Negm §4.2.4: 6:1 / 1:5 / 3:1 weight study already published. Kåge:
  current practice "relies heavily on trial and error"
  Remaining Difference: Reporting the full leakage↔violation Pareto front (Negm
  reported only the best point) + comparison against a constrained formulation
  + continuous severity in metres replacing a binary term
  Strength: Weak as "multi-objective"; moderate as "Pareto reporting + 
  constrained comparison"
  Confidence: High for the overlap
  Risk: 🟠 Medium-High
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Contribution Claim: Generalisation / robustness evaluation
  Closest Prior Work: Cyriac & Chacko 2026 (claims methodology "transferable to
  networks of other sizes and layouts"); gap confirmed by Javed 2025, Kåge 
  2025, Negm 2024, Fu et al. 2022 (Water Research, 420 citations: "no practical
   adoption reported")
  Evidence: Cyriac's abstract asserts transferability. But (this phase): no
  leakage model, no PRV control, one proprietary Abu Dhabi network, and the
  abstract describes a single case study — so the claim reads as an assertion 
  about methodology portability, not a demonstrated  cross-network experiment
  (Inference, Medium-High)
  Remaining Difference: Explicit train/test distribution separation with a 
  held-out Δ and an out-of-phase pattern-break test — neither present in either
   Hu paper (both tested in-distribution) nor evidenced in Cyriac
  Strength: Moderate — and it rests entirely on the experimental protocol
  Confidence: Low-Medium — G-1 full text unread
  Risk: 🔴 High — if Cyriac's full text shows a real cross-network experiment,
  this weakens sharply
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Contribution Claim: Continuous action space for RL pressure control
  Closest Prior Work: Cyriac & Chacko 2026
  Evidence: Cyriac's abstract states prior studies "have overlooked using RL
  pressure control with continuous action spaces" — and then fills that gap
  Remaining Difference: None that is defensible
  Strength: None — do not claim
  Confidence: Medium-High
  Risk: 🔴 Claiming this is now a direct contradiction of a published 2026 ASCE 
  paper
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Contribution Claim: Integrated PPO + EPANET framework
  Closest Prior Work: Belfadil et al. 2023 (DRL-EPANET, ASCE, 10 real networks);

  Negm 2024 (bespoke Python↔EPANET environment); Hu 2023; Tsiami 2025; Cyriac 
  2026 (EPANET + EPyT + Gym + SB3)
  Evidence: Proposal §1 claims no such coherent framework exists; its own §2 and
   
  Appendix 1 cite Negm, who built one
  Remaining Difference: None
  Strength: None — do not claim
  Confidence: High
  Risk: 🔴 Internal contradiction, refutable with one citation in the viva
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 6
  Contribution Claim: Observability-constrained state design and quantifying the

  cost of observability
  Closest Prior Work: Negm 2024 (used nodal leakage rate — not measurable in any

  real network); Belfadil 2023 (used only pressures, tank levels, time)
  Evidence: Negm verbatim: observation includes "nodal leakage rate"; Negm's own

  caveat about sensitivity to incorrect observations. Belfadil verbatim:
  "pressure at demand nodes, tank levels, and time"
  Remaining Difference: Measuring the S2↔S3 gap — the performance cost of
  restricting the state to deployable quantities. Neither Negm nor Belfadil
  measured it
  Strength: Moderate — small but genuine, and protocol-dependent
  Confidence: Medium
  Risk: 🟡 Medium — modest in scale
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 7
  Contribution Claim: Magnitude of the DDA→PDA artifact in DRL pressure control
  Closest Prior Work: No work in the corpus declares a demand model
  Evidence: This phase: \bPDA\b=0, \bDDA\b=0, demand-driven=0 in Negm's full
  text; Phase 1.5-B established the same silence for Hu and Belfadil. Only
  Mahdavi 1400 and Abdy Sayyed 2015 confirm PDA use
  Remaining Difference: Quantifying how much of a reported leakage reduction is 
  supply deficit rather than saving — a number that does not exist in the
  literature
  Strength: Moderate — arguably the cleanest standalone result available
  Confidence: Medium-High
  Risk: 🟢 Low-Medium — depends on A7 producing a non-trivial gap
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 8
  Contribution Claim: Experimental protocol as a whole: adequate budget, ≥5
  seeds, equal tuning parity, held-out distribution, pre-registered
  feasibility-first ranking, full Pareto reporting, ARR
  Closest Prior Work: Negm (20 k steps, 3 evaluation episodes, no seeds
  reported,
  unequal tuning); Hu (3 repetitions, in-distribution tests); Pei et al. 2025
  (unassessed — abstract not retrieved)
  Evidence: Negm verbatim: "identical 20,000 timesteps"; "three episodes"; SZ08
  "not hyperparameter tuned". Proposal and Seminar: seed = 0 occurrences
  Remaining Difference: Above the current standard of this sub-field on every 
  axis — but this is rigour, not conceptual novelty
  Strength: Moderate, and honestly the strongest surviving ground
  Confidence: Medium
  Risk: 🟡 Medium — a reviewer may call it "good practice, not contribution"

  Overall assessment, stated conservatively. This is a combination of known 
  components. No single element is conceptually new. What differentiates the
  work, if anything does, is the experimental protocol — attribution ablations,
  distributional separation, full Pareto reporting, and the DDA-artifact
  quantification. Claims 4 and 5 must be dropped outright. Claim 3 must be
  demoted until G-1 is closed. Claims 6, 7 and 8 are the defensible core.

  Not assessed and therefore an open novelty risk: Pei, Hoang, Fu & Butler 2025
  (ASCE JWRPM 151(6), DRL real-time pump scheduling) — discovered in this phase
  via Cyriac's reference list, abstract not retrievable from Crossref. G8 is now
  demonstrably incomplete: a highly relevant 2025 paper in the field's leading
  journal surfaced only as a side-effect of resolving another paper's
  references. An independent novelty-check is required once search is available.
  I do not claim the 2025–2026 landscape has been covered.
